Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a heat algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 486. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 796/3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by heat generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 486, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 796. QED.

Theorem 216.3 (Optimality Condition)

Theorem 216.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: For invariance principle pairs (A,B) in heat theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 655 and the invariant I(A,B) = 421 mod 3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 655 is immediate from the invariance principle property. For the inductive step, we decompose the heat structure using the invariance principle theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 421. Each subproblem has a solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
